**The landing + the verdicts (law 5, law 16).** Beat 7 LANDED: core `9f99368` (4-exact, pushed) + hivemind `3be8aef` (5-exact, pushed) — both at the pasted porcelains. The gate of record then spoke on `9f99368`: **CI/Build & Check GREEN (2m)** — banks; **install-smoke RED** — banks, permanent, never re-run (law 16). Hub porcelains re-taken fresh this beat (lock-free): hivemind CLEAN at `3be8aef`; core carries exactly one stray line — the stray ruling below.

**The mechanism (adjudicated at the pasted step log — D2; the log is the exhibit).** Gradle+npm SUCCESS (55 jars bundled); the jlink step died. The all-roots jdeps invocation ALSO lists every jar on `--class-path "${IMAGE}/lib/*"`, so each package is seen at the SAME jar path twice — once as a root, once as a class-path member — and jdeps emits a per-jar `Warning: split package: <pkg> <path> <same path>` line **on STDOUT**. The `2>/dev/null` silences stderr only. The `$( )` capture swallowed warnings + module list whole into `JDEPS_MODS`; the `tr ',' '\n' | sed | sort -u | paste -sd,` normalizer folded each warning LINE in as a “module” token (capital `W` collates ahead of every lowercase module name); jlink received garbage tokens in `--add-modules` and died before linking: `java.lang.module.FindException: Module Warning: split package: kotlin.collections.unsigned … not found` — exit 1. **§2b never ran — correctly**: it guards a PRODUCED runtime; jlink's own resolver is an earlier gate of the same family, and it fired first.

**The proof inside the red.** The polluted value's tail carried the REAL union list: `java.base,java.desktop,java.instrument,java.logging,java.management,java.naming,java.net.http,java.security.jgss,java.sql,java.xml,jdk.crypto.cryptoki,jdk.crypto.ec,jdk.jfr,jdk.management,jdk.unsupported,jdk.zipfs` (16 modules) — **`jdk.jfr` PRESENT** (the F-23 payload made it through discovery) plus TWO genuine full-set finds the old single-root invocation never saw (`java.desktop`, `java.instrument`). The R-2 approach is proven; only the capture was dirty.

**Why no earlier gate caught it — and the own.** The desk gate was `bash -n`: syntax only, structurally blind to a tool's runtime stdout behavior. The first live execution of the new invocation was — by design AND by the beat-7 pre-ruling (“commit+push tonight, CI before the card”) — the CI runner: the gate of record fired exactly as sequenced, BEFORE any hardware act. THE HUB OWNS the Layer-2 miss: the audit reasoned over the diff and never asked *“what else does jdeps print on stdout under the new shape?”* Capture hygiene was not in the audit question set. It is now — the harvest row below.

**The fix (ruled: minimal-diff; hub-authored patch artifact per the OR-GATE-M7.5c-a form; Nick applies; census exactly 1 M; NEW commit per law 16).** ONE pipeline stage inserted at the capture: `2>/dev/null | grep -Ex '[a-zA-Z0-9._]+(,[a-zA-Z0-9._]+)*' || true` — a WHITELIST at the expected grammar: only the pure comma-joined module-deps line can enter `JDEPS_MODS`; every warning shape — present or future, self-split or genuine cross-jar — is excluded by grammar, not enumerated; an empty capture degrades to the pre-existing `${JDEPS_MODS:-java.base}` floor-only fallback, whose breakage the downstream smoke asserts catch honestly. `--class-path` deliberately KEPT: minimal diff on a red, and genuine cross-jar splits (the kotlin-stdlib-shim class) would pollute stdout regardless — the filter, not root-set surgery, is the load-bearing fix. Three comment lines teach the mechanism at the site (comment/code unity — the R-2 brief's own discipline). The patch travels as a FILE, not chat bytes (law 11): `_scratch/2026-08-21_build-image_capture-whitelist.patch`, applied `git apply --check` then `git apply` — atomic: applies whole or rejects loud. Hub-side pre-verification: TEST-APPLIED GREEN in-container against a byte-exact stub of the region (git apply + `bash -n` + anchor greps — old line gone, new line single, diffstat +4/−1).

**The gate + the prediction (filed pre-run, D-5 style).** install-smoke on the fix commit = the gate of record. Expected green log: `jlink --add-modules java.base,java.desktop,java.instrument,…,jdk.zipfs` (the 16-module union, warnings absent), §2b `floor-presence assert green: all 16 requested modules present in the runtime`, then checks 1–9 green with check 4 printing an observed events count ≥1 (probe 1) and zero throw-signature hits (probe 2). Green ⇒ bank next beat; Saturday proceeds whole. Red ⇒ paste-back, adjudicate at the hub, never re-run — a runner probe-1 red remains the pre-ruled P6-refutation STOP.

**Saturday impact (the sitting is TOMORROW, Sat Aug 22).** Block 1 UNAFFECTED — the held card runs the OLD artifact (`0.1.0+gd26777c`); the RED evidence leg needs no new build. Block 2 GATED on the fix commit's install-smoke GREEN — and the §OP self-heals: its Block-2 first act is `git pull && git log --oneline -1` (unpinned; the fix rides in). Pi bench deploys unaffected (installDist path — `build-image.sh` is not in it). The token rotation block still rides Block 3 (restore) or the first quiet beat.

**The stray ruling (D14 sweep-guard).** The fresh core porcelain carries `?? _scratch/` — a repo-INSIDE `_scratch/` holding two v49-era commit-msg files (mtimes Aug-8: `2026-08-08_core_lifecycle-mc-bprime-fold_commit-msg.txt`, `commit-msg-m9.5-durd-bprime.txt`) — a pre-session stray from the M9.5-DURd-B′ era, written inside the repo instead of the sibling `_scratch/`. Content benign (msg text already public in git history). RULED: mv-to-sibling (delete-nothing) — ORDER A step 0 moves both files to `../_scratch/` and removes the emptied dir; core porcelain returns to clean; census staging stayed explicit-path throughout, so the stray never threatened an order.

**Harvest row minted (for the next skills/lessons touch, alongside beat-7's).** CAPTURE HYGIENE: any command-substitution capture that feeds a parser (jlink, config, census) is whitelist-filtered to its expected grammar AT the capture site. The audit question is “what else can this tool print on THIS stream under THIS invocation?” Stream redirects and blacklists are not filters of record.

**Next.** The fix landing + the CI verdict paste → bank · Saturday: the card ⏺ intake → then R-3/PKG-E2E-1 authoring (weekend 2) → R-4 re-rep → THE FENCE LIFT · ~Aug-27/28: the branch word (readiness brief standing; on A also the R-1 word) · RS-4 post-anchor · the token rotation block (Saturday Block 3 or the first quiet beat) · pm-handoff rotation at the first quiet beat (~282KB) · (P) standing asks unchanged.

- **Owner:** Nick (launch) / hub (audit; a row for the R-5 charter if the class repeats, its own row if a new mechanism). **Closes when:** the return is audited and the class adjudicated, and the next nightly reads 8/9.
- **Status 2026-09-03 (v61 beat 11) — ADJUDICATED, rows pending.** The evidence read returned and audited ACCEPT (`context/audits/2026-09-03_intake_two-layer-audit_v61-beat-11_s31-read+PKG-SEC-2.md` Part A): H-D — transport collateral from the suite's own `usb-reenumeration` leg (port kill → NCP reset → reopen 6.4 s before the s31 command); the hub verified at source that `attemptReopen()` (:841–:874) calls `resumeStored()` without `awaitNetworkUp()` — the loop resumes before NETWORK_UP and nothing logs the unicast accept/reject. **The rider above is CORRECTED: the 09-03 nightly was NOT missed — it fired late (~11:12Z, a catch-up on the card's return) and PASSED 8/9; the floor is back.** Candidate rows for Nick's word (H10, v62): REOPEN-NETUP (Core) · S31-SETTLE-AFTER-REENUM (bench, at R-5). **Closes when** both rows are worded and the class re-reads 8/9 across the following nights.

#### OR-FAILCHAN — healthy outcomes reported through failure channels (a trust-erosion class) — [OPEN 2026-08-30, v58 beat 6; source: the R-3a hub-return §2.3]
- **The class — four instances in one day, plus the fixed KEYb sibling:** a graceful operator-requested stop exits 143 → systemd marks the unit `failed` (§6-B; root-correct fix = exit 0 on the caught-SIGTERM shutdown path — the record §6 mechanism note; a unit-side `SuccessExitStatus=143` would mask a genuine kill) · an orderly serial close emits `transport_failed` carrying F-S16’s "physical port loss" signature (§10-O) · `key_establishment_failed` for `0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF` (§10-M — CAUSE OPEN; the emission is a faithful 0x009B relay, source-read v58 b6) · a device with no recorded report time renders `Current` (§10-I; read-path). **Never-false-ALIVE guards against claiming health that is not there; nothing guards against claiming failure that is not there — the second error costs operator trust as fast.**
- **Owner:** hub (the sweep WU charters at the September plan; §6-B is the first candidate) / Coder. **Closes when:** the sweep lands and each instance is re-classified or fixed, verified on a card.
- **Status 2026-09-03 (v62 beat 1) — a FIFTH instance, source-verified (the mirror of §6-B): EXITCODE-UNWIRED.** `app/homesynapse-app/src/main/java/com/homesynapse/app/ExitCode.java:25` defines `CONFIGURATION_FAILURE(10)` (+11 PERSISTENCE · 12 EVENT_BUS · 13 SUBSYSTEM_INIT_TIMEOUT · 99 UNEXPECTED) and nothing in main sources references it; `Main.main` is `throws Exception` (:63) with `manager.start()` unwrapped (:148); `HomeSynapseCore.start()` :441–:448 logs the fatal, tears down and re-throws → the JVM exits 1. The unit's `RestartPreventExitStatus=10` (`distribution/systemd/homesynapse.service:66`) therefore never matches and a deterministic bad config restart-loops (`RestartSec=10`) to `StartLimitBurst=5`/300 s — the loop the unit's own comment (:61–:63) says it prevents. Doc 12 C12-04 (:457) is half-built: the structured log line exists, the exit code does not. **Disposition:** rides the FAILCHAN charter (v62 Block 2) as its own classification row; H10 in the v62 brief §G-1 (rec (a): catch at `main`, map FATAL subsystem → `ExitCode`, `System.exit(code)` after teardown). Evidence: `context/audits/2026-09-03_v62-b1_boot-grounding_docs-and-strategy_validation.md` §1.1.

#### OR-JOURNALD-PRIO — journald priority is blind to every application warning — [OPEN 2026-08-30, v58 beat 6; source: the R-3a hub-return §10-P; MECHANISM VERIFIED at source v58 b6]
- **The mechanism (verified):** `homesynapse.service:99–100` routes stdout/stderr → journal; no `sd_journal`/`SyslogLevelPrefix`/priority-prefix emission exists anywhere in app/core/config/distribution — every application line lands at journald’s default INFO priority. Measured: 158–201 app WARN/ERROR lines in one R-3a day; `journalctl -p warning` reported **2**, both systemd’s own. Any priority-filtering monitor sees a healthy service while warnings accumulate.
- **Fix candidates:** `<N>`-prefix the console handler (systemd’s `SyslogLevelPrefix` default honors it) or journal-native logging. Charter at the September plan / the R-10 docket. **Closes when:** app WARN+ lines carry journal priority ≥ warning, proven by a `journalctl -p warning` count on a card.

#### OR-M13-SDNOTIFY — sd_notify real AF_UNIX-datagram transport deferred to M13 (NEW 2026-06-07)
- **Status 2026-08-22 (v56 beat 2) — OPEN; charter-bound.** The R10-IN-L return (`context/audits/2026-08-22_R10-IN-L_liveness-and-notify-transport_return.md` §3/§5) re-priced the M5-D matrix: FFM is final in JDK 22 but LTD-01 forbids preview features, so T-2 is an LTD-01 amendment, not a transport choice; recommendation **T-0 (stay `Type=exec` + the probe; defer M13 indefinitely; the unit's staged flip block is a live trap — rewritten as a DANGER note by R-9 rider R-9-b)**. Resolves at the R-10 charter's R-L2 word (T-0 closes this OR as DEFERRED-BY-DESIGN; any transport word re-opens it as a WU).
- **Severity:** LOW–MEDIUM (a *functional* Tier-1 systemd health reporter needs it; M5-A ships the contract + protocol semantics correctly behind a seam — compile + unit tests GREEN; no MVP consumer is blocked until on-device Tier-1 deployment).
- **Detail:** Pure OpenJDK 21 does not support AF_UNIX `SOCK_DGRAM` — `DatagramChannel.open(StandardProtocolFamily.UNIX)` throws `UnsupportedOperationException` at runtime (JEP 380 delivered Unix-domain *stream* sockets only). `sd_notify` is a datagram protocol, so `SystemdHealthReporter`'s real transport is non-functional on a stock JDK. M5-A isolates this behind the package-private `NotifyTransport` seam (the real transport is never constructed in tests; its default ctor now throws a clear "deferred to M13" `IllegalStateException`).
- **Owner:** PM (decide mechanism) / Coder (implement at M13 composition root). **Resolution condition:** pick the transport mechanism — **JNR/JNA native binding** (needs a version-catalog entry + approval; interacts with the GraalVM native-image decision) **vs a `systemd-notify` subprocess fallback** (watchdog-PID caveats; process-per-heartbeat is poor on a Pi) — **resolved with the M5-D evidence** (the choice is entangled with the native-image spike), then implemented at M13 where the reporter is constructed and `$NOTIFY_SOCKET` resolved. Closed when a working transport ships + a Tier-1 sd_notify integration test passes on-device.
- **M5-D decision matrix AUTHORED 2026-06-07** (`context/assessments/2026-06-07_M5-D_sd_notify_transport_decision_matrix_OR-M13-SDNOTIFY.md`): compares JNR/JNA vs the `systemd-notify` subprocess (+ the FFM/Panama JDK-native option due-diligence adds, + the M5-A "keep the seam, defer" baseline) with the GraalVM native-image entanglement flagged (cites the language-replatform assessment §1.2/§3.1/§4.1). **PM recommendation:** **defer the final commit to M13, gated on the M5-D GraalVM spike + the WatchdogSec decision + the JDK-version trajectory** — **reject JNR/JNA** (dominated: new dep + worst native-image fit for a 3-syscall job); **subprocess if no WATCHDOG keepalives** (zero-dep, closed-world-clean), else **FFM/Panama** (zero-dep, in-process, native-image-friendly; needs JDK 22+ / `--enable-preview`). **No catalog dependency added now.** Two items surfaced for Nick: (i) the GraalVM C15 spike should report whether JNR/JNA-class reflection bindings survive its closed-world config; (ii) **decide whether HomeSynapse opts into the systemd watchdog (WatchdogSec)** — a Doc 12 lifecycle choice that largely settles FFM-vs-subprocess.
